```
## Profiler / flat-file cleanup: timeout drift, duplicated format+stat logic, N+1 profiler queries

Low-priority cleanup items surfaced during the full `/code-review` of `58193cd..HEAD` (column profiler + check runners, #137–#144). Grouped here; none is a correctness bug. The correctness/robustness findings are tracked separately in #145, and the dispatch/registry gap in #146.

### 1. S3 read-timeout drift (30s → 60s)
When the flat-file download was extracted into `flatfile.py`, the S3 read timeout changed from the profiler's original 30s (`profile_service._NETWORK_TIMEOUT`) to [`_READ_TIMEOUT = 60`](backend/app/datasources/flatfile.py#L38). More lenient than before; a hung S3 read now ties up a worker thread twice as long. Confirm 60s is intentional (full-file reads may legitimately need longer than a profiler sample) vs accidental drift, and align the two constants.

### 2. `_SUPPORTED_FORMATS` duplicates `format_from_path`
[`infer_file_format`](backend/app/services/profile_service.py#L333) calls `flatfile.format_from_path(path)` then checks membership in [`_SUPPORTED_FORMATS = {"csv","parquet"}`](backend/app/services/profile_service.py#L62). But `format_from_path` already only ever returns `csv`/`parquet`/`None`, so the supported-format set is expressed twice. Add JSON to `format_from_path` and the profiler silently still rejects it. Derive the supported set from `format_from_path`, or drop the redundant check.

### 3. Duplicated stat contract: SQL vs pandas
The per-column statistic definitions (null_fraction = nulls/row_count with the row_count==0 guard, distinct excludes nulls, top_values = non-null desc by count) are implemented twice with no shared code — once in SQL via [`assemble_profile`](backend/app/services/profile_service.py#L195) and once in pandas via [`profile_dataframe`](backend/app/services/profile_service.py#L362). A definitional change must be made in both or the two datasource families disagree for the same logical data. (Hard to fully DRY across SQL vs pandas, but the *contract* could be captured in one place / a shared test.)

### 4. N+1 warehouse round-trips in the SQL profiler
[`profile_table`](backend/app/services/profile_service.py#L304) issues 1 aggregate query + one top-values query **per column** in a Python loop. For a 50-column profile that's 51 sequential warehouse queries; against a cold SQL warehouse (~1s/query) that's ~50s of wall time for an interactive authoring aid. Acceptable for v1 (≤50-col cap), but consider batching the top-values queries (UNION ALL / a single windowed query) if profiler latency becomes a problem.

### 5. Parquet footer read twice (minor)
[`_read_dataframe`](backend/app/services/profile_service.py#L440) reads `pq.ParquetFile(raw).schema.names` to filter requested columns to those present, then re-parses with `pd.read_parquet(columns=present)`. The footer read is cheap, and it's needed so `read_parquet` doesn't raise on an absent column — but the column-presence logic now lives in two places (the pre-read filter and `profile_dataframe`'s missing-column 422 check). Worth a glance to ensure they can't diverge.

Pick up opportunistically; suggest a single cleanup PR (`Fixes #<this>`).
=============== #317 ===============
## Move barStatus() into resultsFormat.ts next to RUN_STATUS_COLORS

Follow-up from the /code-review of PR #315 (live run-progress drawer, A3).

`barStatus()` in [LiveRunProgress.tsx](frontend/src/components/runs/LiveRunProgress.tsx) is a parallel, hand-maintained switch over the `RunStatus` union (run lifecycle → antd `Progress` status). It lives apart from the rest of the run-status presentation in [resultsFormat.ts](frontend/src/components/results/resultsFormat.ts), which exists precisely to be the framework-free, unit-tested home for these status→presentation mappings (`RUN_STATUS_COLORS`, `pipelineStatusColor`, …).

**Action:** move `barStatus` into `resultsFormat.ts` next to `RUN_STATUS_COLORS` so the run-status mappings sit together and are reviewed/added-to as one unit, and give it the unit-test treatment that module documents as its reason to exist. Pure refactor, no behavior change.

Note: a new `RunStatus` value currently falls through `barStatus`'s if-chain to `'normal'` silently — consider a `Record<RunStatus, …>` for exhaustiveness while moving it.
```

```
1	import type { ResultStatus, RunStatus } from '../../api/runs';
2	
3	/**
4	 * Pure presentation helpers for the Results surface — kept framework-free so the
5	 * status→colour mapping and the timing formatters can be unit-tested without
6	 * rendering antd.
7	 */
8	
9	/** antd Tag colour per run execution status. */
10	export const RUN_STATUS_COLORS: Record<RunStatus, string> = {
11	  queued: 'default',
12	  running: 'processing',
13	  succeeded: 'success',
14	  failed: 'error',
15	  cancelled: 'warning',
16	};
17	
18	/** antd Tag colour per result severity / operational status (ADR 0005 / #122). */
19	export const RESULT_STATUS_COLORS: Record<ResultStatus, string> = {
20	  pass: 'success',
21	  warn: 'warning',
22	  fail: 'error',
23	  critical: 'magenta',
24	  skip: 'default',
25	  error: 'volcano',
26	};
27	
28	/** Orchestrator pipeline-run status → colour (provider-agnostic value set). */
29	export function pipelineStatusColor(status: string): string {
30	  switch (status) {
31	    case 'succeeded':
32	      return 'success';
33	    case 'failed':
34	      return 'error';
35	    case 'running':
36	      return 'processing';
37	    case 'cancelled':
38	      return 'warning';
39	    default:
40	      return 'default';
41	  }
42	}
43	
44	/**
45	 * Render an unknown scalar (a GX observed/expected value, a profiled min/max or
46	 * top value) for display: an em dash for null/undefined, JSON for objects, the
47	 * `String` form otherwise. Falsy scalars (`0`, `false`, `''`) render as
48	 * themselves — not collapsed to the em dash — so a real zero isn't mistaken for
49	 * "no value". The em-dash sentinel matches `formatTimestamp` / `formatDuration`.
50	 */
51	export function formatScalar(value: unknown): string {
52	  if (value === null || value === undefined) return '—';
53	  return typeof value === 'object' ? JSON.stringify(value) : String(value);
54	}
55	
56	/** Absolute timestamp as a locale string, or an em dash when absent. */
57	export function formatTimestamp(iso: string | null): string {
58	  if (!iso) return '—';
59	  const d = new Date(iso);
60	  return Number.isNaN(d.getTime()) ? '—' : d.toLocaleString();
61	}
62	
63	/**
64	 * Run duration (finished − started) as a compact human string: `850ms`, `12s`,
65	 * `1m 3s`. Returns an em dash when either bound is missing (queued / never
66	 * finished) or the interval is negative (clock skew).
67	 */
68	export function formatDuration(startedAt: string | null, finishedAt: string | null): string {
69	  if (!startedAt || !finishedAt) return '—';
70	  const ms = new Date(finishedAt).getTime() - new Date(startedAt).getTime();
71	  if (Number.isNaN(ms) || ms < 0) return '—';
72	  if (ms < 1000) return `${ms}ms`;
73	  const totalSeconds = Math.round(ms / 1000);
74	  if (totalSeconds < 60) return `${totalSeconds}s`;
75	  const minutes = Math.floor(totalSeconds / 60);
76	  const seconds = totalSeconds % 60;
77	  return `${minutes}m ${seconds}s`;
78	}
79	
```

```
1	import {
2	  App,
3	  Alert,
4	  Button,
5	  Drawer,
6	  Empty,
7	  Flex,
8	  List,
9	  Progress,
10	  Spin,
11	  Tag,
12	  Typography,
13	} from 'antd';
14	import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';
15	import { Link } from 'react-router-dom';
16	
17	import {
18	  cancelRun,
19	  getRunProgress,
20	  type ResultStatus,
21	  type RunProgress,
22	  type RunStatus,
23	} from '../../api/runs';
24	import { RESULT_STATUS_COLORS, RUN_STATUS_COLORS } from '../results/resultsFormat';
25	
26	/** Run lifecycle states past which polling stops. */
27	const TERMINAL: readonly RunStatus[] = ['succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled'];
28	const DEFAULT_POLL_MS = 1500;
29	
30	function isTerminal(status: RunStatus): boolean {
31	  return TERMINAL.includes(status);
32	}
33	
34	/** antd Progress bar status from the run lifecycle. */
35	function barStatus(status: RunStatus): 'success' | 'exception' | 'active' | 'normal' {
36	  if (status === 'succeeded') return 'success';
37	  if (status === 'failed' || status === 'cancelled') return 'exception';
38	  if (status === 'running') return 'active';
39	  return 'normal';
40	}
41	
42	/**
43	 * Live run-progress drawer — opens on a queued run and polls
44	 * `GET /runs/{id}/progress` until the run is terminal, showing the run
45	 * lifecycle, a completed/total bar, and per-check status (a spinner while a
46	 * check is still pending). An editor can cancel an in-flight run.
47	 *
48	 * Mounted controlled by `runId`; pass `null` to keep it closed. The body is
49	 * keyed by `runId` so opening a different run remounts and restarts polling
50	 * rather than continuing the prior run's loop.
51	 */
52	export function LiveRunProgress({
53	  runId,
54	  suiteName,
55	  canManage,
56	  pollMs = DEFAULT_POLL_MS,
57	  onClose,
58	}: {
59	  runId: string | null;
60	  suiteName: string | null;
61	  canManage: boolean;
62	  /** Poll interval; overridable so tests can drive it deterministically. */
63	  pollMs?: number;
64	  onClose: () => void;
65	}) {
66	  return (
67	    <Drawer
68	      open={runId !== null}
69	      onClose={onClose}
70	      width={560}
71	      title={`Run progress${suiteName ? ` · ${suiteName}` : ''}`}
72	      destroyOnClose
73	    >
74	      {runId !== null && (
75	        <LiveRunProgressBody key={runId} runId={runId} canManage={canManage} pollMs={pollMs} />
76	      )}
77	    </Drawer>
78	  );
79	}
80	
81	function LiveRunProgressBody({
82	  runId,
83	  canManage,
84	  pollMs,
85	}: {
86	  runId: string;
87	  canManage: boolean;
88	  pollMs: number;
89	}) {
90	  const { message } = App.useApp();
91	  const [progress, setProgress] = useState<RunProgress | null>(null);
92	  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
93	  const [cancelling, setCancelling] = useState(false);
94	  const timerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
95	  // `stopped` latches once the run reaches a terminal state (poll-observed or
96	  // cancel-forced). It guards against a poll that was already in flight before a
97	  // cancel resolving afterwards and clobbering the terminal status back to
98	  // `running` (cancel is cooperative — the next poll can briefly still read the
99	  // pre-cancel state). Terminal is sticky.
100	  const stoppedRef = useRef(false);
101	
102	  // Poll until terminal via a self-scheduling timeout (not setInterval, so a slow
103	  // request can't pile up overlapping fetches). `active` guards a late resolution
104	  // after unmount. A transient fetch error keeps polling (the live view
105	  // self-heals when the endpoint recovers) — only a terminal status stops it.
106	  useEffect(() => {
107	    let active = true;
108	    stoppedRef.current = false;
109	    const tick = async () => {
110	      try {
111	        const next = await getRunProgress(runId);
112	        if (!active || stoppedRef.current) return;
113	        setProgress(next);
114	        setError(null);
115	        if (isTerminal(next.status)) {
116	          stoppedRef.current = true;
117	        } else {
118	          timerRef.current = setTimeout(tick, pollMs);
119	        }
120	      } catch (err) {
121	        if (!active || stoppedRef.current) return;
122	        setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));
123	        // Keep polling through a transient error rather than freezing the live
124	        // view; the cadence is bounded by pollMs, and a terminal status / unmount
125	        // still stops it.
126	        timerRef.current = setTimeout(tick, pollMs);
127	      }
128	    };
129	    void tick();
130	    return () => {
131	      active = false;
132	      if (timerRef.current) clearTimeout(timerRef.current);
133	    };
134	  }, [runId, pollMs]);
135	
136	  const onCancel = async () => {
137	    setCancelling(true);
138	    try {
139	      const run = await cancelRun(runId);
140	      // Stop polling and latch terminal so an in-flight pre-cancel poll can't
141	      // flip the status back to `running`.
142	      stoppedRef.current = true;
143	      if (timerRef.current) clearTimeout(timerRef.current);
144	      setProgress((p) => (p ? { ...p, status: run.status, finished_at: run.finished_at } : p));
145	      message.success('Run cancelled');
146	    } catch (err) {
147	      message.error(`Cancel failed: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'unknown error'}`);
148	    } finally {
149	      setCancelling(false);
150	    }
151	  };
152	
153	  if (!progress) {
154	    if (error) {
155	      return (
156	        <Alert type="error" showIcon message="Failed to load run progress" description={error} />
157	      );
158	    }
159	    return <Spin tip="Starting run…" size="large" />;
160	  }
161	
162	  const { status, total_checks, completed_checks, checks } = progress;
163	  const terminal = isTerminal(status);
164	  const percent = total_checks > 0 ? Math.round((completed_checks / total_checks) * 100) : 0;
165	
166	  return (
167	    <Flex vertical gap={16}>
168	      <Flex gap={12} align="center" wrap>
169	        <Tag color={RUN_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>
170	        <Typography.Text type="secondary">
171	          {completed_checks} / {total_checks} checks
172	        </Typography.Text>
173	        {canManage && !terminal && (
174	          <Button danger size="small" loading={cancelling} onClick={onCancel}>
175	            Cancel
176	          </Button>
177	        )}
178	      </Flex>
179	
180	      <Progress percent={percent} status={barStatus(status)} />
181	
182	      {/* A transient poll error while we still have prior progress to show. */}
183	      {error && (
184	        <Alert type="warning" showIcon message="Progress update failed" description={error} />
185	      )}
186	
187	      {checks.length === 0 ? (
188	        <Empty description="This suite has no checks to run." />
189	      ) : (
190	        <List<(typeof checks)[number]>
191	          size="small"
192	          dataSource={checks}
193	          rowKey="check_id"
194	          renderItem={(c) => (
195	            <List.Item>
196	              <Typography.Text>{c.name}</Typography.Text>
197	              <CheckStatus status={c.status} terminal={terminal} />
198	            </List.Item>
199	          )}
200	        />
201	      )}
202	
203	      {/* Always offer the persistent results surface — the drawer can be closed
204	          mid-run, and (unlike the old navigate-on-run) it's the only in-app path
205	          back to this run until the recent-runs table lands. */}
206	      <Link to="/results">View full results →</Link>
207	    </Flex>
208	  );
209	}
210	
211	/**
212	 * A check's status cell: a resolved check shows its severity tag; a pending
213	 * check spins *while the run is live*, but on a terminal run (a check that never
214	 * produced a result — e.g. a cancelled run) it shows a neutral "not run" rather
215	 * than an eternal spinner.
216	 */
217	function CheckStatus({ status, terminal }: { status: ResultStatus | null; terminal: boolean }) {
218	  if (status === null) {
219	    if (terminal) {
220	      return <Typography.Text type="secondary">not run</Typography.Text>;
221	    }
222	    return (
223	      <Flex gap={6} align="center">
224	        <Spin size="small" />
225	        <Typography.Text type="secondary">pending</Typography.Text>
226	      </Flex>
227	    );
228	  }
229	  return <Tag color={RESULT_STATUS_COLORS[status]}>{status}</Tag>;
230	}
231	
```

```
1	import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
2	
3	import {
4	  formatDuration,
5	  formatScalar,
6	  formatTimestamp,
7	  pipelineStatusColor,
8	  RESULT_STATUS_COLORS,
9	  RUN_STATUS_COLORS,
10	} from '../../src/components/results/resultsFormat';
11	
12	describe('formatScalar', () => {
13	  it('returns an em dash for null or undefined', () => {
14	    expect(formatScalar(null)).toBe('—');
15	    expect(formatScalar(undefined)).toBe('—');
16	  });
17	
18	  it('renders falsy scalars as themselves, not the em dash', () => {
19	    expect(formatScalar(0)).toBe('0');
20	    expect(formatScalar(false)).toBe('false');
21	    expect(formatScalar('')).toBe('');
22	  });
23	
24	  it('JSON-stringifies objects and arrays', () => {
25	    expect(formatScalar({ a: 1 })).toBe('{"a":1}');
26	    expect(formatScalar([1, 2])).toBe('[1,2]');
27	  });
28	
29	  it('stringifies plain scalars', () => {
30	    expect(formatScalar('PUBLIC')).toBe('PUBLIC');
31	    expect(formatScalar(9999)).toBe('9999');
32	  });
33	});
34	
35	describe('formatDuration', () => {
36	  it('returns an em dash when either bound is missing', () => {
37	    expect(formatDuration(null, '2026-06-11T00:00:10Z')).toBe('—');
38	    expect(formatDuration('2026-06-11T00:00:00Z', null)).toBe('—');
39	    expect(formatDuration(null, null)).toBe('—');
40	  });
41	
42	  it('returns an em dash for a negative interval (clock skew)', () => {
43	    expect(formatDuration('2026-06-11T00:00:10Z', '2026-06-11T00:00:00Z')).toBe('—');
44	  });
45	
46	  it('formats sub-second, seconds, and minute+second spans', () => {
47	    expect(formatDuration('2026-06-11T00:00:00.000Z', '2026-06-11T00:00:00.850Z')).toBe('850ms');
48	    expect(formatDuration('2026-06-11T00:00:00Z', '2026-06-11T00:00:12Z')).toBe('12s');
49	    expect(formatDuration('2026-06-11T00:00:00Z', '2026-06-11T00:01:03Z')).toBe('1m 3s');
50	  });
51	});
52	
53	describe('formatTimestamp', () => {
54	  it('returns an em dash for null or unparseable input', () => {
55	    expect(formatTimestamp(null)).toBe('—');
56	    expect(formatTimestamp('not-a-date')).toBe('—');
57	  });
58	
59	  it('returns a non-empty locale string for a valid ISO timestamp', () => {
60	    const out = formatTimestamp('2026-06-11T00:00:00Z');
61	    expect(out).not.toBe('—');
62	    expect(out.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
63	  });
64	});
65	
66	describe('status colour maps', () => {
67	  it('maps every run status and result status to a colour', () => {
68	    expect(RUN_STATUS_COLORS.succeeded).toBe('success');
69	    expect(RUN_STATUS_COLORS.failed).toBe('error');
70	    expect(RESULT_STATUS_COLORS.warn).toBe('warning');
71	    expect(RESULT_STATUS_COLORS.critical).toBe('magenta');
72	  });
73	
74	  it('maps pipeline statuses with a default fallback', () => {
75	    expect(pipelineStatusColor('succeeded')).toBe('success');
76	    expect(pipelineStatusColor('failed')).toBe('error');
77	    expect(pipelineStatusColor('something-new')).toBe('default');
```

```
1	"""Column profiler — per-column statistics for the check editor.
2	
3	Given a target (a SQL table or a flat file) and a set of columns on a suite's
4	connection, compute the stats an author needs before writing expectations: row
5	count, null count / fraction, distinct count, min / max, and the most frequent
6	values. Persists nothing — a read-only authoring aid (the check-editor "profile
7	on table/file select" panel).
8	
9	`profile_connection` dispatches on the connection type:
10	
11	* **SQL datasources** (Snowflake + Unity Catalog) — aggregate the stats
12	  in-warehouse with one round-trip + a top-values query per column, via the
13	  datasource's SQLAlchemy dialect. Unity Catalog adds a `catalog` so the table is
14	  qualified `catalog.schema.table` (3-level namespace); Snowflake is `schema.table`.
15	* **Flat-file datasources** (ADLS Gen2, S3) — download a *sample* of the file
16	  (`_SAMPLE_ROWS` rows) into Pandas and compute the same stats locally. CSV and
17	  Parquet are supported; stats are therefore over the sample, not the whole file.
18	
19	**SQL-injection safety.** For SQL datasources, table / schema / column names are
20	caller-supplied and become SQL *identifiers* (they can't be bound parameters).
21	Queries are built with the SQLAlchemy Core expression language (`select` /
22	`table` / `column`) — never string formatting — so the dialect does the quoting
23	and there is no raw-SQL sink. As defence-in-depth (and a clean early 422) each
24	identifier is also validated against a strict allowlist. Flat-file columns are
25	checked for existence against the loaded frame instead (a missing column is a
26	clean 422, and Pandas indexing never builds SQL).
27	
28	Like the GX adapter, the pure pieces (identifier validation, query building,
29	dataframe profiling, result assembly) are unit-testable without a live
30	datasource; the I/O seams (`_open_connection`, `_read_dataframe`) are
31	monkeypatched in tests, and a live smoke is deferred.
32	"""
33	
34	from __future__ import annotations
35	
36	import io
37	import math
38	import re
39	from collections.abc import Callable, Generator, Mapping
40	from contextlib import contextmanager
41	from dataclasses import dataclass
42	from typing import Any
43	
44	from sqlalchemy import column, distinct, func, quoted_name, select, table
45	from sqlalchemy.sql import Select
46	
47	from backend.app.core.errors import DataQError
48	from backend.app.core.jsonsafe import sanitize_json
49	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
50	from backend.app.core.secrets import SecretStore
51	from backend.app.datasources.flatfile import download_bytes, format_from_path
52	from backend.app.datasources.snowflake import (
53	    SnowflakeConfig,
54	    build_connect_args,
55	    build_connection_string,
56	)
57	from backend.app.datasources.unity_catalog import UnityCatalogConfig, build_databricks_url
58	from backend.app.db.models import Connection
59	
60	log = get_logger(__name__)
61	
62	# Formats the profiler can actually parse. NOT redundant with
63	# flatfile.format_from_path (which only recognises path extensions): this also
64	# validates the caller's `explicit` file_format override (an arbitrary string),
65	# and is deliberately a *subset* of recognised formats — a format can be
66	# recognised by path yet unsupported here, which should still 422 (#147).
67	_SUPPORTED_FORMATS = {"csv", "parquet"}
68	# Flat-file profiling reads at most this many rows — stats are over the sample.
69	_SAMPLE_ROWS = 100_000
70	
71	# A plain SQL identifier: letter/underscore start, then letters/digits/_/$ (the
72	# Snowflake unquoted-identifier set). Anything else (spaces, quotes, dots, etc.)
73	# is refused — it can't be made injection-safe by quoting alone here.
74	_IDENTIFIER = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*$")
75	
76	# Connector timeouts (seconds): fail fast rather than hang the request thread.
77	_LOGIN_TIMEOUT = 10
78	_NETWORK_TIMEOUT = 30
79	
80	
81	class ProfileUnsupportedError(DataQError):
82	    status_code = 422
83	    code = "profile_unsupported"
84	
85	
86	class ProfileTargetInvalidError(DataQError):
87	    status_code = 422
88	    code = "profile_target_invalid"
89	
90	
91	class ProfileIdentifierInvalidError(DataQError):
92	    status_code = 422
93	    code = "profile_identifier_invalid"
94	
95	
96	class ProfileColumnNotFoundError(DataQError):
97	    status_code = 422
98	    code = "profile_column_not_found"
99	
100	
101	class ProfileFailedError(DataQError):
102	    status_code = 502
103	    code = "profile_failed"
104	
105	
106	@dataclass(frozen=True)
107	class ColumnProfile:
108	    column: str
109	    null_count: int
110	    null_fraction: float
111	    distinct_count: int | None  # None when the column's values aren't hashable
112	    min_value: Any
113	    max_value: Any
114	    top_values: list[dict[str, Any]]  # [{"value": ..., "count": int}]
115	
116	
117	@dataclass(frozen=True)
118	class ProfileResult:
119	    """A profiled target. Identity fields are type-specific: SQL datasources set
120	    `table` / `schema`, flat-file datasources set `path` / `file_format`."""
121	
122	    row_count: int
123	    columns: list[ColumnProfile]
124	    table: str | None = None
125	    schema: str | None = None
126	    catalog: str | None = None
127	    path: str | None = None
128	    file_format: str | None = None
129	
130	
131	# ───────────────────────── pure query builders ─────────────────────
132	
133	
134	def validate_identifier(name: str | None) -> str:
135	    """Validate `name` against the plain-identifier allowlist and return it.
136	
137	    Raises `ProfileIdentifierInvalidError` (422) for anything that isn't a plain
138	    identifier. The SQLAlchemy Core builders quote safely on their own; this is
139	    defence-in-depth and turns an odd name into a clean 422 instead of a quoted
140	    column that simply doesn't exist.
141	    """
142	    if not name or not _IDENTIFIER.match(name):
143	        raise ProfileIdentifierInvalidError(
144	            "not a valid table/schema/column identifier", detail={"identifier": name}
145	        )
146	    return name
147	
148	
149	def _table(schema: str, table_name: str, catalog: str | None = None) -> Any:
150	    """A Core table clause, optionally with a 3-level namespace (Unity Catalog).
151	
152	    With a `catalog`, the namespace is `catalog.schema` passed as an unquoted
153	    `quoted_name` so the dialect emits three dotted parts (`catalog.schema.table`)
154	    rather than quoting the dotted string as one identifier. Safe because every
155	    part is allowlist-validated.
156	    """
157	    validate_identifier(schema)
158	    if catalog is not None:
159	        validate_identifier(catalog)
160	        namespace: Any = quoted_name(f"{catalog}.{schema}", quote=False)
161	    else:
162	        namespace = schema
163	    return table(validate_identifier(table_name), schema=namespace)
164	
165	
166	def build_aggregate_query(
167	    schema: str, table_name: str, columns: list[str], catalog: str | None = None
168	) -> Select[Any]:
169	    """One round-trip: row count + null/distinct/min/max per column.
170	
171	    Built with the Core expression language (no string SQL); identifiers are
172	    validated then handed to `column()`/`table()`, which the dialect quotes.
173	    """
174	    projection: list[Any] = [func.count().label("row_count")]
175	    for i, col in enumerate(columns):
176	        c: Any = column(validate_identifier(col))
177	        projection.append((func.count() - func.count(c)).label(f"nulls_{i}"))
178	        projection.append(func.count(distinct(c)).label(f"distinct_{i}"))
179	        projection.append(func.min(c).label(f"min_{i}"))
180	        projection.append(func.max(c).label(f"max_{i}"))
181	    return select(*projection).select_from(_table(schema, table_name, catalog))
182	
183	
184	def build_top_values_query(
185	    schema: str, table_name: str, col: str, top_n: int, catalog: str | None = None
186	) -> Select[Any]:
187	    """Most frequent non-null values for one column (highest count first)."""
188	    c: Any = column(validate_identifier(col))
189	    freq = func.count().label("freq")
190	    return (
191	        select(c.label("value"), freq)
192	        .select_from(_table(schema, table_name, catalog))
193	        .where(c.is_not(None))
194	        .group_by(c)
195	        .order_by(func.count().desc(), c)
196	        .limit(int(top_n))
197	    )
198	
199	
200	def assemble_profile(
201	    *,
202	    table: str,
203	    schema: str,
204	    columns: list[str],
205	    aggregate: Mapping[str, Any],
206	    top_values: dict[str, list[Mapping[str, Any]]],
207	    catalog: str | None = None,
208	) -> ProfileResult:
209	    """Build the `ProfileResult` from raw query rows (pure, warehouse-free)."""
210	    row_count = int(aggregate["row_count"])
211	    profiles: list[ColumnProfile] = []
212	    for i, col in enumerate(columns):
213	        nulls = int(aggregate[f"nulls_{i}"])
214	        profiles.append(
215	            ColumnProfile(
216	                column=col,
217	                null_count=nulls,
218	                null_fraction=(nulls / row_count) if row_count else 0.0,
219	                distinct_count=int(aggregate[f"distinct_{i}"]),
220	                min_value=sanitize_json(aggregate[f"min_{i}"]),
221	                max_value=sanitize_json(aggregate[f"max_{i}"]),
222	                top_values=[
223	                    {"value": sanitize_json(r["value"]), "count": int(r["freq"])}
224	                    for r in top_values.get(col, [])
225	                ],
226	            )
227	        )
228	    return ProfileResult(
229	        table=table, schema=schema, catalog=catalog, row_count=row_count, columns=profiles
230	    )
231	
232	
233	# ───────────────────────── profiler registry ───────────────────────
234	#
235	# One table maps connection.type to its profiling strategy, so adding a
236	# datasource is a single entry here — not edits scattered across the type sets,
237	# `_engine_args`, and `profile_connection` (#146). SQL types carry their engine
238	# builder + whether they need a `catalog`; flat-file types are uniform (the
239	# object-store backend, S3 vs ADLS, is dispatched inside `flatfile`).
240	
241	
242	def _snowflake_engine_args(connection: Connection, secret: str) -> tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]:
243	    sf = SnowflakeConfig.model_validate(connection.config)
244	    return build_connection_string(sf, secret), {
245	        "login_timeout": _LOGIN_TIMEOUT,
246	        "network_timeout": _NETWORK_TIMEOUT,
247	        # Key-pair auth threads the private key in as a connect-arg (empty for password).
248	        **build_connect_args(sf, secret),
249	    }
250	
251	
252	def _unity_catalog_engine_args(connection: Connection, secret: str) -> tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]:
253	    cfg = UnityCatalogConfig.model_validate(connection.config)
254	    # Catalog is not pinned on the URL — the profiler query qualifies the full
255	    # catalog.schema.table namespace itself (see `_table`).
256	    return build_databricks_url(cfg, secret), {}
257	
258	
259	@dataclass(frozen=True)
260	class _SqlProfiler:
261	    """SQL profiling strategy: in-warehouse aggregation over a SQLAlchemy engine."""
262	
263	    engine_args: Callable[[Connection, str], tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]]
264	    requires_catalog: bool = False
265	
266	
267	@dataclass(frozen=True)
268	class _FileProfiler:
269	    """Flat-file profiling strategy: sample into pandas (backend handled by flatfile)."""
270	
271	
272	_Profiler = _SqlProfiler | _FileProfiler
273	
274	_PROFILERS: dict[str, _Profiler] = {
275	    "snowflake": _SqlProfiler(_snowflake_engine_args),
276	    "unity_catalog": _SqlProfiler(_unity_catalog_engine_args, requires_catalog=True),
277	    "s3": _FileProfiler(),
278	    "adls_gen2": _FileProfiler(),
279	}
280	
281	
282	# ───────────────────────── I/O seam (monkeypatched in tests) ────────
283	
284	
285	def _engine_args(connection: Connection, secret: str) -> tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]:
286	    """Build the (SQLAlchemy URL, connect_args) for a SQL datasource connection."""
287	    profiler = _PROFILERS.get(connection.type)
288	    if not isinstance(profiler, _SqlProfiler):
289	        raise ProfileUnsupportedError(
290	            f"{connection.type!r} is not a SQL profiling datasource",
291	            detail={"type": connection.type},
292	        )
293	    return profiler.engine_args(connection, secret)
294	
295	
296	@contextmanager
297	def _open_connection(connection: Connection, secret_store: SecretStore) -> Generator[Any]:
298	    """Yield a live SQLAlchemy connection to the datasource, disposing the engine."""
299	    from sqlalchemy import create_engine
300	
301	    if not connection.secret_ref:
302	        raise ValueError("connection requires secret_ref for the credential")
303	    secret = secret_store.get(connection.secret_ref)
304	    url, connect_args = _engine_args(connection, secret)
305	    engine = create_engine(url, connect_args=connect_args)
306	    try:
307	        with engine.connect() as conn:
308	            yield conn
309	    finally:
310	        engine.dispose()
311	
312	
313	# ───────────────────────── orchestration ───────────────────────────
314	
315	
316	def profile_table(
317	    connection: Connection,
318	    *,
319	    table: str,
320	    schema: str | None,
321	    columns: list[str],
322	    top_n: int,
323	    secret_store: SecretStore,
324	    catalog: str | None = None,
325	) -> ProfileResult:
326	    """Profile `columns` of a SQL `table` on `connection` (dispatched here for
327	    SQL datasource types). `catalog` qualifies the namespace for Unity Catalog
328	    (`catalog.schema.table`); Snowflake leaves it `None`.
329	
330	    Raises `ProfileIdentifierInvalidError` (422) for a bad catalog/schema/table/
331	    column name (validated *before* any query runs), and `ProfileFailedError`
332	    (502) if the profile can't execute — the adapter exception is never echoed
333	    (it can carry DSN/credential fragments).
334	    """
335	    effective_schema = schema if schema is not None else connection.config.get("schema")
336	    if not isinstance(effective_schema, str):
337	        raise ProfileIdentifierInvalidError(
338	            "no schema given and the connection has none", detail={"schema": effective_schema}
339	        )
340	    # Validate every identifier up front (422) before any query is built/run.
341	    if catalog is not None:
342	        validate_identifier(catalog)
343	    validate_identifier(table)
344	    validate_identifier(effective_schema)
345	    for col in columns:
346	        validate_identifier(col)
347	
348	    try:
349	        with _open_connection(connection, secret_store) as conn:
350	            aggregate = (
351	                conn.execute(build_aggregate_query(effective_schema, table, columns, catalog))
352	                .mappings()
353	                .one()
354	            )
355	            top_values = {
356	                col: list(
357	                    conn.execute(
358	                        build_top_values_query(effective_schema, table, col, top_n, catalog)
359	                    ).mappings()
360	                )
361	                for col in columns
362	            }
363	    except Exception as exc:
364	        log.warning(
365	            "column_profile_failed", connection_type=connection.type, error_type=type(exc).__name__
366	        )
367	        raise ProfileFailedError(
368	            "column profile could not execute against the datasource", detail={"table": table}
369	        ) from exc
370	
371	    return assemble_profile(
372	        table=table,
373	        schema=effective_schema,
374	        catalog=catalog,
375	        columns=columns,
376	        aggregate=aggregate,
377	        top_values=top_values,
378	    )
379	
380	
381	# ───────────────────────── flat-file profiling ─────────────────────
382	
383	
384	def infer_file_format(path: str, explicit: str | None) -> str:
385	    """Resolve the file format from an explicit value or the path extension.
386	
387	    Raises `ProfileTargetInvalidError` (422) for an unknown/unsupported format —
388	    the caller can always pass `file_format` to override extension guessing. The
389	    extension mapping is shared with the runner (`flatfile.format_from_path`).
390	    """
391	    fmt = explicit or format_from_path(path)
392	    if fmt not in _SUPPORTED_FORMATS:
393	        raise ProfileTargetInvalidError(
394	            "cannot determine a supported file format; pass file_format",
395	            detail={"path": path, "supported": sorted(_SUPPORTED_FORMATS)},
396	        )
397	    return fmt
398	
399	
400	def _to_native(value: Any) -> Any:
401	    """Coerce a numpy/pandas scalar to a JSON-friendly Python value."""
402	    if value is None:
403	        return None
404	    if hasattr(value, "isoformat"):  # Timestamp / datetime / date
405	        return value.isoformat()
406	    if hasattr(value, "item"):  # numpy scalar → Python scalar
407	        value = value.item()
408	    if isinstance(value, float) and math.isnan(value):
409	        return None
410	    if isinstance(value, bool | int | float | str):
411	        return value
412	    # Anything else a column can hold (bytes/binary, Decimal, UUID, …) → a display
413	    # string, so a min/max/top value is always JSON-encodable, never a 500 at the
414	    # response boundary. `bool` is matched above `int` since bool is an int.
415	    return str(value)
416	
417	
418	def profile_dataframe(
419	    df: Any, *, columns: list[str], top_n: int, path: str, file_format: str
420	) -> ProfileResult:
421	    """Compute per-column stats from an in-memory dataframe (pure, no I/O).
422	
423	    Raises `ProfileColumnNotFoundError` (422) if a requested column isn't in the
424	    frame — a clean error instead of a KeyError 500.
425	    """
426	    missing = [c for c in columns if c not in df.columns]
427	    if missing:
428	        raise ProfileColumnNotFoundError(
429	            "requested column(s) not in the file",
430	            detail={"missing": missing, "available": [str(c) for c in df.columns][:50]},
431	        )
432	    row_count = len(df)
433	    profiles = [_profile_series(col, df[col], row_count=row_count, top_n=top_n) for col in columns]
434	    return ProfileResult(path=path, file_format=file_format, row_count=row_count, columns=profiles)
435	
436	
437	def _profile_series(column: str, series: Any, *, row_count: int, top_n: int) -> ColumnProfile:
438	    """Per-column stats, degrading a messy column to nulls instead of 500-ing.
439	
440	    `null_count` is always computable, but a real-world flat file can hold a
441	    column the stats can't process: min/max raise on **uncomparable** mixed types
442	    (e.g. ints and strings in one object column), and distinct/value_counts raise
443	    on **unhashable** cells (nested list/dict values from Parquet). Each best-effort
444	    stat is guarded independently — and broadly, since the exception type varies by
445	    backend (a numpy object column raises `TypeError`, a pyarrow-backed Parquet
446	    list/struct column raises `ArrowNotImplementedError`) — so one bad column yields
447	    null stats for itself rather than failing the whole profile request.
448	    """
449	    null_count = int(series.isna().sum())
450	    non_null = series.dropna()
451	    try:
452	        minimum = _to_native(non_null.min()) if len(non_null) else None
453	        maximum = _to_native(non_null.max()) if len(non_null) else None
454	    except Exception:
455	        minimum = maximum = None
456	    try:
457	        distinct: int | None = int(non_null.nunique())
458	    except Exception:
459	        distinct = None
460	    try:
461	        counts = non_null.value_counts().head(top_n)
462	        top = [
463	            {"value": sanitize_json(_to_native(value)), "count": int(count)}
464	            for value, count in counts.items()
465	        ]
466	    except Exception:
467	        top = []
468	    return ColumnProfile(
469	        column=column,
470	        null_count=null_count,
471	        null_fraction=(null_count / row_count) if row_count else 0.0,
472	        distinct_count=distinct,
473	        min_value=sanitize_json(minimum),
474	        max_value=sanitize_json(maximum),
475	        top_values=top,
476	    )
477	
478	
479	def _read_dataframe(
480	    connection: Connection,
481	    *,
482	    path: str,
483	    file_format: str,
484	    columns: list[str],
485	    secret_store: SecretStore,
486	) -> Any:
487	    """Download `path` from the flat-file datasource into a sampled dataframe.
488	
489	    The live I/O seam (download + parse) — monkeypatched in tests. Applies the two
490	    "load less data" levers from the pandas scaling guide:
491	
492	    * **column projection** — only the requested `columns` are parsed (CSV
493	      `usecols`, Parquet `columns=`), so profiling 3 of 200 columns doesn't read
494	      all 200. Unknown names are simply not selected; `profile_dataframe` then
495	      reports genuinely-missing ones as a clean 422.
496	    * **row sampling** — at most `_SAMPLE_ROWS` rows (CSV pushes the cap into the
497	      parser; Parquet is sliced after the projected read).
498	
499	    Not done (deliberate, for an authoring-time sampler): streaming/range reads —
500	    the whole object is still downloaded before parsing — and out-of-core engines
501	    (Dask). Both are future work if a profiling-cost problem actually shows up.
502	    """
503	    import pandas as pd
504	
505	    if not connection.secret_ref:
506	        raise ValueError("connection requires secret_ref for the credential")
507	    secret = secret_store.get(connection.secret_ref)
508	    wanted = set(columns)
509	    raw = io.BytesIO(
510	        download_bytes(
511	            conn_type=connection.type, config=connection.config, path=path, secret=secret
512	        )
513	    )
514	    if file_format == "csv":
515	        return pd.read_csv(raw, nrows=_SAMPLE_ROWS, usecols=lambda name: name in wanted)
516	
517	    import pyarrow.parquet as pq
518	
519	    available = set(pq.ParquetFile(raw).schema.names)
520	    raw.seek(0)
521	    present = [c for c in columns if c in available]
522	    # Parquet is already Arrow on disk; dtype_backend="pyarrow" keeps the buffers
523	    # zero-copy instead of materialising a numpy copy. The stat helpers + the
524	    # _to_native coercion are Arrow-scalar-safe (min/max → Python int/str,
525	    # timestamps → Timestamp.isoformat, NA dropped before reductions).
526	    return pd.read_parquet(raw, columns=present, dtype_backend="pyarrow").head(_SAMPLE_ROWS)
527	
528	
529	def profile_file(
530	    connection: Connection,
531	    *,
532	    path: str,
533	    file_format: str | None,
534	    columns: list[str],
535	    top_n: int,
536	    secret_store: SecretStore,
537	) -> ProfileResult:
538	    """Profile `columns` of a flat file on `connection` (ADLS Gen2 / S3).
539	
540	    Raises `ProfileTargetInvalidError` (422) for an unknown format,
541	    `ProfileColumnNotFoundError` (422) for a missing column, and
542	    `ProfileFailedError` (502) if the file can't be read — the underlying
543	    exception is never echoed (it can carry credential/endpoint fragments).
544	    """
545	    fmt = infer_file_format(path, file_format)
546	    try:
547	        df = _read_dataframe(
548	            connection, path=path, file_format=fmt, columns=columns, secret_store=secret_store
549	        )
550	    except Exception as exc:
551	        log.warning(
552	            "column_profile_failed", connection_type=connection.type, error_type=type(exc).__name__
553	        )
554	        raise ProfileFailedError(
555	            "column profile could not read the file", detail={"path": path}
556	        ) from exc
557	
558	    return profile_dataframe(df, columns=columns, top_n=top_n, path=path, file_format=fmt)
559	
560	
561	def profile_connection(
562	    connection: Connection,
563	    *,
564	    columns: list[str],
565	    top_n: int,
566	    table: str | None = None,
567	    schema: str | None = None,
568	    catalog: str | None = None,
569	    path: str | None = None,
570	    file_format: str | None = None,
571	    secret_store: SecretStore,
572	) -> ProfileResult:
573	    """Dispatch to the SQL or flat-file profiler based on the connection type.
574	
575	    Raises `ProfileUnsupportedError` (422) for a type with no profiler, and
576	    `ProfileTargetInvalidError` (422) if the target for that type is missing
577	    (a SQL type needs `table`; Unity Catalog also needs `catalog`; a flat-file
578	    type needs `path`) or the connection has no stored credential.
579	    """
580	    profiler = _PROFILERS.get(connection.type)
581	    if profiler is None:
582	        raise ProfileUnsupportedError(
583	            f"column profiling is not supported for {connection.type!r} connections in v1",
584	            detail={"type": connection.type, "supported": sorted(_PROFILERS)},
585	        )
586	    # A supported connection with no credential is a config problem (422), not a
587	    # transient datasource failure — catch it before the adapter raises a bare
588	    # ValueError that the read/connect guard would relabel as a 502.
589	    if not connection.secret_ref:
590	        raise ProfileTargetInvalidError(
591	            "connection has no stored credential (secret_ref) to profile with",
592	            detail={"type": connection.type},
593	        )
594	    if isinstance(profiler, _SqlProfiler):
595	        if not table:
596	            raise ProfileTargetInvalidError(
597	                "table is required to profile a SQL datasource", detail={"type": connection.type}
598	            )
599	        if profiler.requires_catalog and not catalog:
600	            raise ProfileTargetInvalidError(
601	                "catalog is required to profile a Unity Catalog table",
602	                detail={"type": connection.type},
603	            )
604	        return profile_table(
605	            connection,
606	            table=table,
607	            schema=schema,
608	            catalog=catalog,
609	            columns=columns,
610	            top_n=top_n,
611	            secret_store=secret_store,
612	        )
613	    if not path:
614	        raise ProfileTargetInvalidError(
615	            "path is required to profile a flat-file datasource",
616	            detail={"type": connection.type},
617	        )
618	    return profile_file(
619	        connection,
620	        path=path,
621	        file_format=file_format,
622	        columns=columns,
623	        top_n=top_n,
624	        secret_store=secret_store,
625	    )
626
```

```
1	"""Flat-file (ADLS Gen2 / S3) IO + GX `CheckRunner`.
2	
3	Two responsibilities for the flat-file datasources, both behind the same
4	primitives the SQL adapters use (raw config dict + resolved secret, never the ORM
5	row — keeps `datasources/` decoupled from `db/`):
6	
7	* **IO** — `download_bytes` fetches an object/blob; `read_dataframe` parses it
8	  into pandas. Shared by the column profiler (service layer) and the runner.
9	* **Runner** — `FlatFileCheckRunner` runs GX expectations against the file by
10	  loading it into an in-memory pandas DataFrame and handing that to GX's pandas
11	  datasource, then mapping the result via the shared `gx_runner` machinery. The
12	  `CheckRunner` interface is table-shaped; for a flat-file datasource the
13	  ``table`` argument carries the **file path** and ``schema`` is unused.
14	
15	GX runs entirely in-process on the DataFrame, so — unlike the warehouse runners —
16	the run path is fully testable with a canned frame; only the network download is
17	the deferred-smoke seam.
18	"""
19	
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import io
23	import re
24	from dataclasses import dataclass
25	from datetime import UTC, datetime
26	from typing import Any
27	
28	import great_expectations as gx
29	
30	from backend.app.core.secrets import SecretStore
31	from backend.app.datasources.adls import AdlsConfig
32	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome
33	from backend.app.datasources.gx_runner import run_expectations
34	from backend.app.datasources.s3 import S3Config
35	
36	# Connector timeouts (seconds): fail fast rather than hang the worker thread.
37	# _READ_TIMEOUT is deliberately longer than the SQL profiler's network timeout
38	# (profile_service._NETWORK_TIMEOUT = 30): it covers a full-object download (the
39	# whole CSV/Parquet is pulled before parsing), not a single warehouse query, so a
40	# large file legitimately needs more headroom. Not accidental drift (#147).
41	_CONNECT_TIMEOUT = 10
42	_READ_TIMEOUT = 60
43	
44	_FILE_TYPES = {"adls_gen2", "s3"}
45	
46	# Sort floor for files the store reports without a modified time.
47	_MIN_DT = datetime.min.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
48	
49	
50	def format_from_path(path: str) -> str | None:
51	    """Infer the file format from the path extension (`None` if unrecognised)."""
52	    lower = path.lower()
53	    if lower.endswith(".csv"):
54	        return "csv"
55	    if lower.endswith((".parquet", ".pq")):
56	        return "parquet"
57	    return None
58	
59	
60	def _s3_client(cfg: S3Config, secret: str) -> Any:
61	    """A boto3 S3 client for `cfg` with the standard fail-fast timeouts."""
62	    import boto3
63	    from botocore.config import Config
64	
65	    return boto3.client(
66	        "s3",
67	        region_name=cfg.region,
68	        aws_access_key_id=cfg.access_key_id,
69	        aws_secret_access_key=secret,
70	        config=Config(connect_timeout=_CONNECT_TIMEOUT, read_timeout=_READ_TIMEOUT),
71	    )
72	
73	
74	def _blob_service(acfg: AdlsConfig, secret: str) -> Any:
75	    """An ADLS `BlobServiceClient` for `acfg` (caller must `.close()` it)."""
76	    from azure.storage.blob import BlobServiceClient
77	
78	    return BlobServiceClient(account_url=acfg.account_url, credential=secret)
79	
80	
81	def download_bytes(*, conn_type: str, config: dict[str, Any], path: str, secret: str) -> bytes:
82	    """Fetch the object/blob bytes from S3 or ADLS Gen2 (live seam).
83	
84	    Takes the connection's `type` + raw `config` + resolved `secret` (the caller
85	    owns the SecretStore), so this module never touches the DB.
86	    """
87	    if conn_type == "s3":
88	        cfg = S3Config.model_validate(config)
89	        body: bytes = _s3_client(cfg, secret).get_object(Bucket=cfg.bucket, Key=path)["Body"].read()
90	        return body
91	
92	    acfg = AdlsConfig.model_validate(config)
93	    client_az = _blob_service(acfg, secret)
94	    try:
95	        blob = client_az.get_blob_client(container=acfg.container, blob=path)
96	        downloaded: bytes = blob.download_blob().readall()
97	        return downloaded
98	    finally:
99	        client_az.close()
100	
101	
102	def read_dataframe(*, conn_type: str, config: dict[str, Any], path: str, secret: str) -> Any:
103	    """Download and parse the **whole** file into a pandas DataFrame (live seam).
104	
105	    The runner reads the full file — every row and column — because a check may
106	    reference any column and row-count/null checks must be exact (unlike the
107	    profiler, which samples + projects). Raises `ValueError` for an unknown
108	    format.
109	    """
110	    import pandas as pd
111	
112	    fmt = format_from_path(path)
113	    if fmt is None:
114	        raise ValueError(f"unsupported flat-file format for path {path!r}")
115	    raw = io.BytesIO(download_bytes(conn_type=conn_type, config=config, path=path, secret=secret))
116	    if fmt == "csv":
117	        return pd.read_csv(raw)
118	    return pd.read_parquet(raw, dtype_backend="pyarrow")
119	
120	
121	class FlatFileCheckRunner:
122	    """`CheckRunner` for flat files — loads the file into pandas, runs GX on it.
123	
124	    Holds the resolved credential (like `SnowflakeCheckRunner` holds its
125	    connection string), so `run_checks` is self-contained. ``table`` is the file
126	    path; ``schema`` is ignored (flat files have no schema namespace).
127	    """
128	
129	    def __init__(self, *, conn_type: str, config: dict[str, Any], secret: str) -> None:
130	        self._conn_type = conn_type
131	        self._config = config
132	        self._secret = secret
133	
134	    def run_checks(
135	        self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[CheckSpec]
136	    ) -> SuiteOutcome:
137	        df = read_dataframe(
138	            conn_type=self._conn_type, config=self._config, path=table, secret=self._secret
139	        )
140	        context = gx.get_context(mode="ephemeral")
141	        asset = context.data_sources.add_pandas(name="flatfile").add_dataframe_asset(name="file")
142	        # The pandas asset takes its batch at run time via batch_parameters; the
143	        # ephemeral context makes the fixed suite/vd names safe across runs.
144	        batch_definition = asset.add_batch_definition_whole_dataframe(name="whole_dataframe")
145	        return run_expectations(
146	            context,
147	            batch_definition=batch_definition,
148	            checks=checks,
149	            name="suite-flatfile",
150	            batch_parameters={"dataframe": df},
151	        )
152	
153	
154	def build_flatfile_runner(
155	    *, conn_type: str, config: dict[str, Any], secret_ref: str | None, secret_store: SecretStore
156	) -> FlatFileCheckRunner:
157	    """Build a runner from a flat-file `Connection`'s primitives.
158	
159	    Mirrors `build_snowflake_runner`: resolves the secret eagerly and takes the
160	    raw config (not the ORM model) to keep the adapter decoupled from `db/`.
161	    """
162	    if conn_type not in _FILE_TYPES:
163	        raise ValueError(f"{conn_type!r} is not a flat-file datasource")
164	    if not secret_ref:
165	        raise ValueError("flat-file connection requires secret_ref for the credential")
166	    secret = secret_store.get(secret_ref)
167	    return FlatFileCheckRunner(conn_type=conn_type, config=config, secret=secret)
168	
169	
170	# ───────────────────────── batch resolution ────────────────────────
171	#
172	# Flat files usually arrive in batches — `orders_2026-06-01.csv`,
173	# `orders_2026-06-02.csv`, … — and a check targets *one* batch. The batch
174	# pattern is a regex whose **first capture group is the batch key**; `latest`
175	# selects the greatest key, `specific` selects a named key. Resolution (filter +
176	# select) is pure and fully tested; only the object listing is a live seam.
177	
178	
179	class BatchNotFoundError(ValueError):
180	    """No file matched the batch pattern (or the requested specific batch)."""
181	
182	
183	@dataclass(frozen=True)
184	class FileRef:
185	    """A listed object: its full key/blob path and last-modified time (if any)."""
186	
187	    path: str
188	    last_modified: datetime | None = None
189	
190	
191	def _most_recent(files: list[FileRef]) -> str:
192	    """Path of the most recently modified file (ties broken by path; `files` non-empty)."""
193	    return max(files, key=lambda f: (f.last_modified or _MIN_DT, f.path)).path
194	
195	
196	def resolve_batch(
197	    files: list[FileRef], *, pattern: str, strategy: str = "latest", batch: str | None = None
198	) -> str:
199	    """Pick one file's path from `files` per the batch `pattern` + `strategy`.
200	
201	    `pattern` is a regex `re.search`-ed against each path; its first capture group
202	    (if any) is the batch key. `strategy`:
203	
204	    * ``latest`` — the greatest batch key (lexicographic — ISO dates sort right),
205	      or, when the pattern has no capture group, the most recently modified file.
206	    * ``specific`` — the file whose batch key equals `batch` (required).
207	
208	    Raises `BatchNotFoundError` (nothing matched / no such batch) or `ValueError`
209	    (bad strategy, or `specific` without `batch`).
210	    """
211	    try:
212	        compiled = re.compile(pattern)
213	    except re.error as exc:
214	        raise ValueError(f"invalid batch pattern {pattern!r}: {exc}") from exc
215	    matches = [(f, m) for f in files if (m := compiled.search(f.path))]
216	    if not matches:
217	        raise BatchNotFoundError(f"no files matched batch pattern {pattern!r}")
218	
219	    if strategy == "specific":
220	        if batch is None:
221	            raise ValueError("strategy 'specific' requires a batch key")
222	        hits = [f for f, m in matches if m.groups() and m.group(1) == batch]
223	        if not hits:
224	            raise BatchNotFoundError(f"no file for batch {batch!r} under pattern {pattern!r}")
225	        return _most_recent(hits)
226	
227	    if strategy != "latest":
228	        raise ValueError(f"unknown batch strategy {strategy!r}")
229	
230	    # A batch key is the first capture group when it *participated* in the match;
231	    # an optional group that didn't (`None`) has no key, so it falls through to the
232	    # modified-time ordering rather than crashing the `max` on a None vs str compare.
233	    keyed = [(f, m.group(1)) for f, m in matches if m.groups() and m.group(1) is not None]
234	    if keyed:
235	        return max(keyed, key=lambda fk: fk[1])[0].path
236	    return _most_recent([f for f, _ in matches])
237	
238	
239	def list_files(
240	    *, conn_type: str, config: dict[str, Any], prefix: str, secret: str
241	) -> list[FileRef]:
242	    """List objects/blobs under `prefix` on a flat-file datasource (live seam)."""
243	    if conn_type == "s3":
244	        cfg = S3Config.model_validate(config)
245	        paginator = _s3_client(cfg, secret).get_paginator("list_objects_v2")
246	        refs: list[FileRef] = []
247	        for page in paginator.paginate(Bucket=cfg.bucket, Prefix=prefix):
248	            for obj in page.get("Contents", []):
249	                refs.append(FileRef(path=obj["Key"], last_modified=obj.get("LastModified")))
250	        return refs
251	
252	    acfg = AdlsConfig.model_validate(config)
253	    client_az = _blob_service(acfg, secret)
254	    try:
255	        container = client_az.get_container_client(acfg.container)
256	        return [
257	            FileRef(path=blob.name, last_modified=getattr(blob, "last_modified", None))
258	            for blob in container.list_blobs(name_starts_with=prefix)
259	        ]
260	    finally:
261	        client_az.close()
262	
263	
264	def resolve_batch_file(
265	    *,
266	    conn_type: str,
267	    config: dict[str, Any],
268	    secret: str,
269	    prefix: str,
270	    pattern: str,
271	    strategy: str = "latest",
272	    batch: str | None = None,
273	) -> str:
274	    """List under `prefix`, then resolve the batch file path (list + `resolve_batch`)."""
275	    files = list_files(conn_type=conn_type, config=config, prefix=prefix, secret=secret)
276	    return resolve_batch(files, pattern=pattern, strategy=strategy, batch=batch)
277
```

```json
{
  "command": "gh issue create \\\n  --title \"perf(profiler): batch the N+1 per-column top-values warehouse queries\" \\\n  --milestone \"Backlog (post-v1 / testing)\" \\\n  --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nSplit out of #147 (item 4) so that issue can close — the other four cleanup items are resolved/confirmed there.\n\n`profile_table` ([profile_service.py](backend/app/services/profile_service.py)) issues 1 aggregate query + **one top-values query per column** in a Python loop. For a 50-column profile that's 51 sequential warehouse round-trips; against a cold SQL warehouse (~1s/query) that's ~50s of wall time for an interactive authoring aid.\n\nAcceptable for v1 (≤50-col cap, and the aggregate query is a single round-trip), so this is **post-v1 perf**, not a correctness bug.\n\n### Why not done now\n- Explicitly conditional in #147: \"consider batching … **if profiler latency becomes a problem**.\"\n- A safe rewrite (e.g. `UNION ALL` of per-column grouped+limited subqueries, one round-trip) mixes differently-typed `value` columns into one projection, which coerces per-dialect — genuinely risky across both Snowflake and Databricks, and **live warehouse runs are still the deferred Week-1 smoke**, so there's no integration test to catch a regression.\n\n### When to pick up\nOnce a live warehouse smoke exists (so the batched SQL can be validated end-to-end), or if profiler latency is reported in practice. Keep the per-column builder as the fallback for dialects where the batched form misbehaves.\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "File backlog issue for profiler N+1"
}
```

**#147 was a 5-item grab bag** — here's the honest disposition rather than a blanket "done":
- **Items 1 & 2** (S3 timeout, `_SUPPORTED_FORMATS`) — already resolved in #164; the rationale comments referencing #147 are in the code. No change needed.
- **Item 3** (duplicated stat contract) — ✅ fixed (shared `null_fraction`; distinct/top-values can't share code across SQL/pandas, so they stay pinned by parallel tests).
- **Item 4** (N+1 per-column top-values queries) — real but explicitly-deferred perf, and risky to rewrite (type-mixing across Snowflake/Databricks) with no live-warehouse smoke to catch a regression. **Filed as [#327](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/327)** (Backlog) rather than silently dropped, and `Fixes #147` references it.
- **Item 5** (parquet column-presence in two places) — consistent by construction (absent → dropped → reported missing → 422). No change.

> AGENT

This is a trivial, behavior-preserving refactor — three small hunks. I carefully traced the mapping equivalence rather than spawning 8 finder agents for a 3-line change:

**Old `barStatus`:** `succeeded→success`, `failed|cancelled→exception`, `running→active`, else (`queued`)→`normal`.
**New `RUN_BAR_STATUS`:** `queued→normal`, `running→active`, `succeeded→success`, `failed→exception`, `cancelled→exception`.

These are identical for all 5 `RunStatus` values. The `Record` form is strictly safer (exhaustive). The test covers all 5 keys; the single call site is updated correctly.

```
+# ───────────────────────── shared stat contract ────────────────────

+def null_fraction(null_count: int, row_count: int) -> float:
+    """Fraction of rows that are null, guarding the empty-target divide-by-zero.
+
+    The one stat definition the SQL profiler (`assemble_profile`) and the pandas
+    profiler (`profile_dataframe`) can actually share — both must agree that a
+    0-row target reports `0.0`, not `1.0` or a `ZeroDivisionError` (#147). The
+    other contract points (distinct excludes nulls; top values are non-null,
+    highest-count-first) are structurally SQL-vs-pandas and can't share code, so
+    they're pinned by the parallel-path tests instead.
+    """
+    return (null_count / row_count) if row_count else 0.0
+
+
```

```
75	> **Auth-boundary discipline (per [ADR 0010](adr/0010-provider-agnostic-infrastructure-seams.md)):** new connection-CRUD endpoints (and every protected route from here on) depend on a generic internal "current user" dependency that returns DataQ's own `User` — they must NOT read MSAL token claims directly in route/service code. Cheap now; expensive to retrofit once dozens of endpoints exist. No new abstraction layer required, just the boundary.

Enforces `(type, env)` uniqueness for orchestrator rows via a **partial unique index** `WHERE type IN ('adf','airflow')` per [#72](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/72) / ADR 0004 — datasources excluded, so Snowflake stays many-per-env. CRUD/API reused unchanged: pure adapter + registry + migration addition.)_
80	- [x] ✅ Connection re-auth endpoint — refresh expired Key Vault token — `POST /connections/{id}/reauth` (`svc.reauth_connection`): rotates the credential through `SecretStore.set` **and** verifies it via the same adapter probe as `/test`, in one step (the gap PATCH+`/test` leave open). Rotation persists before the probe, so a bad new credential surfaces as 502 `connection_test_failed`; a store-write failure is 502 `connection_secret_write_failed` with the old credential untouched. 6 TestClient tests (rotate+verify ok, failed-verify-but-rotation-persists, write-fail 502, 404, secret-required 422). Type-agnostic — applies to all six connection types

Recorded as a comment on the `secret_ref` column in `db/models.py`

115	- [x] ✅ API: CRUD for suites and GX expectations (Snowflake path) — **suites** (PR-B1): `suite_service` + `/suites` CRUD (`connection_id` validated then immutable; delete cascades to checks). **checks** (PR-B2): `check_service` + nested `/suites/{id}/checks` CRUD surfacing `kind` + `warn/fail/critical_threshold` + GX `expectation_type`/`config`. v1 monitor-kind guard (only `expectation`; reserved kinds → 422, ADR 0012); checks scoped to their suite (cross-suite access → 404); thresholds are `Decimal` in (exact `Numeric` storage) / `float` out (clean JSON). 24 TestClient tests; all four modules 100%. Share-based access filtering deferred to the suite-sharing task; **DQ-dimension classification** deferred + tracked ([#124](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/124))
116	- [x] ✅ API: suite sharing — assign users with owner / editor / viewer roles — **sharing API + authz core** (PR-E1): `suite_authz.require_permission` (404-hides a suite with no access, 403s an insufficient level) + `share_service` + `/suites/{id}/shares` CRUD. Schema vocab `view`/`edit`/`admin` + implicit owner=`created_by`; **admin can delete + manage shares** (per decision); grant-to-owner/unknown → 422; manage needs `admin`, list needs `view`. **Enforcement** (PR-E2): `require_permission` applied across the suite endpoints (GET=view, PATCH=edit, DELETE=admin) + all check endpoints (reads=view, writes=edit); `list_suites` scoped to owned-or-shared. Lands the access control deferred in B1/B2. ~26 TestClient tests across the matrix acting as different users (viewer reads-not-writes, editor writes-not-deletes, admin deletes, outsider→404, list scoping); shares/suites/checks routes + share_service all 100%
117	- [x] ✅ API: suite export to JSON + import from JSON — `suite_io_service` + `GET /suites/{id}/export` (view) / `POST /suites/import` (any authed user, like create). Document is **connection-agnostic** — omits all DB identity (`id`/`connection_id`/`created_by`/timestamps), so it's a reusable template; import **re-binds** to a freshly chosen `connection_id` and owns the new suite as the importer. Round-trippable: thresholds are `Decimal` in/out (exact). Import is **atomic** — every check kind is validated before any row is written (bad doc → 422, nothing persisted); unknown `version` → 422; missing connection → 422. Checks emitted in stable creation order (diffable). Reuses `check_service.validate_kind` (no dup). 7 TestClient tests (no-identity-leak, view-gated, owned-by-importer, export→import→export round-trip, unknown-connection/version/kind-atomic); `suite_io_service` + `suites.py` 100%

Reuses the severity derivation (ADR 0005/0016) + JSON sanitiser. `require_permission` **edit** (authoring); table passed in the body (checks don't carry a target table yet). v1 → 422: non-`expectation` kind, non-Snowflake connection (runner dispatch generalises Week 5); execution failure → 502 (adapter exception never echoed). No `sample_failures` in the preview (PII; follow-up). 7 TestClient tests (mocked runner); `dryrun_service` + `checks.py` 100%

121	> **Day 1 design decision: severity weights — ✅ settled in [ADR 0005](adr/0005-severity-tier-weights.md) (warn 0.5 / fail 1.0 / critical 2.0; health = 100×(1−Σpenalty/(N×2.0))).**

Thresholds band the GX **unexpected-%** as `metric_value` (higher=worse, ordered, unset-tier skipped); thresholds-as-policy override GX `success`; binary fallback when no thresholds / no metric. **Settled in [ADR 0016](adr/0016-severity-derivation-semantics.md)** (incl. A→B reversibility: raw `observed_value` retained → switch is additive `direction` column + backfill, never destructive). `duration_ms` stays NULL (per-check timing not separable from GX's suite-level `validate()`). 16 unit + 1 integration test; both modules 100%

133	### Column profiler (3 tasks — 3/3 ✅)
134	- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (Snowflake) — nulls, distinct count, min / max, top values — `profile_service` + `POST /suites/{id}/profile` (require_permission **edit**, suite-scoped so the connection is access-gated). Reads-only, persists nothing: one aggregate query (row count + null/distinct/min/max per column) + one top-N-values query per column, then `assemble_profile`. **SQL-injection-safe** — queries are built with the **SQLAlchemy Core expression language** (`select`/`table`/`column`, dialect-quoted) so there's no raw-string SQL sink (no S608/B608/CodeQL `py/sql-injection`); identifiers are additionally allowlist-validated (`validate_identifier`, strict `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*$`) as defence-in-depth + a clean early 422, and `top_n` is `int()`-coerced. v1 → 422: non-Snowflake type (dispatch generalises Week 5), bad identifier, no schema; execution failure → 502 (adapter exception never echoed). min/max/top-values NaN-sanitised. 28 tests (22 pure: identifier allowlist incl. injection strings, compiled-SQL builders, assembly, div-by-zero, NaN; 6 endpoint via fake conn: stats, injection-422, unsupported-type-422, 502, edit-gated, no-schema-422); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 90% (live `_open_connection` is the deferred warehouse seam)
135	- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (ADLS / S3) — same stats via Pandas on sampled file — extended `profile_service` into a type dispatcher (`profile_connection`): SQL types → in-warehouse aggregation (unchanged), flat-file types (`adls_gen2`/`s3`) → download a **sample** of the file into Pandas and compute the same stats (`profile_dataframe`). Same `POST /suites/{id}/profile` endpoint, now polymorphic: SQL targets pass `table`/`schema`, flat-file targets pass `path` (+ optional `file_format`, else inferred from extension); response carries whichever identity applies. CSV + Parquet. **pandas "load less data" levers** (per the user's scale.html prompt): column projection (CSV `usecols`, Parquet `columns=` via pyarrow schema) so profiling 3 of N columns doesn't read all N, + row sampling (`_SAMPLE_ROWS`), + **zero-copy Arrow-backed dtypes** on the Parquet read (`dtype_backend="pyarrow"` — parquet is already Arrow, kept zero-copy; the stat helpers + `_to_native` are Arrow-scalar/`pd.NA`-safe). Stats are vectorised pandas (no Python row loops — enhancingperf N/A; CoW is a no-op for a read-only profiler + a process-global flag, so not toggled; pyarrow CSV engine rejected — it's incompatible with `nrows`/callable-`usecols`). NaN/Timestamp→JSON-safe. v1 → 422: unsupported type (UC still pending), missing target, unknown format, missing column; read failure → 502 (exception never echoed). Reuses `ColumnProfile`/`ProfileResult`. 24 new tests (pure `profile_dataframe`/`infer_file_format`/`_to_native`/projection-parse + endpoint via fake `_read_dataframe`); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 85% (only the live `_open_connection`/`_download_bytes` network seams uncovered — deferred smoke). _Deferred: streaming/range reads (whole object still downloaded before sampling) + out-of-core (Dask) — future work if profiling cost bites._
136	- [x] ✅ Column profiler endpoint (Unity Catalog) — via Databricks SQL Warehouse — added `unity_catalog` to the profiler's **SQL** branch (reuses `profile_table` + the SQLAlchemy Core query builders + `assemble_profile` — no duplicate aggregation logic). `_open_connection` now dispatches the engine by type (`_engine_args`): Snowflake URL or a `REDACTED` URL via the installed `databricks` SQLAlchemy dialect. UC's **3-level namespace** (`catalog.schema.table`) is handled by building the table with a `quoted_name(f"{catalog}.{schema}", quote=False)` so the dialect emits three dotted parts rather than quoting the dotted string as one — verified by compiling the real builders against the Databricks dialect. Injection-safe: every part (catalog/schema/table/column) is allowlist-validated (no raw-string SQL, no CodeQL py/sql-injection surface). Endpoint gained `catalog` (request + response); the dispatcher requires it for UC (`422 profile_target_invalid` if absent). 12 new tests (catalog-aware builders compiled to SQL, `_engine_args` URL dispatch, UC endpoint stats via the same fake conn, missing-catalog 422, bad-catalog-identifier 422); `suites.py` 100%, `profile_service` 86% (only the live `_open_connection`/`_download_bytes` network seams uncovered — deferred smoke). **Completes the column profiler across all 4 datasources.**

138	### Flat file check specifics (2 tasks — 2/2 ✅)
139	- [x] ✅ Check types for flat files: schema validation, row count, null checks, ~~freshness by filename date~~ — `FlatFileCheckRunner` (`datasources/flatfile.py`): downloads the file (S3/ADLS) into a pandas DataFrame and runs the suite's GX expectations against GX's **pandas DataFrame asset**, so all GX expectation-kind checks (schema/row-count/null/value checks) run against flat files. Extracted the shared GX machinery — snake_case→GX-class translation, GX-result→`SuiteOutcome` mapping, and the suite/validation-definition `run_expectations` flow — into `datasources/gx_runner.py` (reused by the Snowflake runner and the future UC runner; no duplication). Flat-file IO (`download_bytes` + full-file `read_dataframe`) moved to `flatfile.py` behind primitives (raw config + resolved secret, not the ORM — matches `build_snowflake_runner`), and the column profiler now shares it. Because GX runs **in-process on the DataFrame**, the run path is fully unit-tested with a canned frame (real GX execution, not mocked) — only the network download is the deferred-smoke seam. 16 new tests; `gx_runner` 100%, `flatfile` 76% (only `download_bytes` uncovered). _Connection-type → runner dispatch (Snowflake vs flat-file) is the Week-5 `run_suite` wiring; **freshness-by-filename** is the reserved `freshness` monitor kind (deferred, ADR 0012), not a GX expectation._
140	- [x] ✅ Batch resolution — resolve batching regex to matched files, pick latest or specific batch — `resolve_batch` (`datasources/flatfile.py`): a batch `pattern` is a regex whose **first capture group is the batch key**; `latest` selects the greatest key (lexicographic — ISO dates sort chronologically), falling back to the storage modified-time when the pattern has no group; `specific` selects the file whose key equals a given `batch`. `BatchNotFoundError` on no match / unknown batch. The filter+select logic is pure and fully unit-tested (8 tests); object listing (`list_files` — S3 `list_objects_v2` paginated / ADLS `list_blobs`) is the live seam, and `resolve_batch_file` composes list+resolve. _Wiring a check's target to a resolved batch path is the Week-5 `run_suite` dispatch._
141	
142	### Unity Catalog check specifics (2 tasks — 2/2 ✅)
143	- [x] ✅ UC table check path — UC table → GX DataFrame datasource → run suite — `UnityCatalogCheckRunner` (`datasources/unity_catalog.py`): reads the target table from the Databricks SQL Warehouse into a pandas DataFrame (`pd.read_sql_table` via the databricks SQLAlchemy dialect — reflection quotes identifiers, so no hand-built SQL) and runs the suite's GX expectations against GX's **pandas DataFrame asset** — the "GX DataFrame datasource" shape (§5), the same shape Databricks Labs DQX consumes, so v1.1 swaps GX→DQX behind this one interface. Reuses the shared `gx_runner` (`run_expectations` + result mapping) exactly like the flat-file runner; the `catalog` is pinned in the connection URL (`build_databricks_url`, now shared with the column profiler — no duplication) so the 2-level `schema.table` resolves to `catalog.schema.table`. Because GX runs **in-process on the DataFrame**, the run path is unit-tested with a canned frame (real GX execution); only the reflect+read (`_read_table`) is the deferred-smoke seam. `build_unity_catalog_runner` mirrors `build_snowflake_runner` (raw config + resolved PAT, not the ORM). 14 tests; `unity_catalog` 91% (only `_read_table` uncovered). _Connection-type → runner dispatch is the Week-5 `run_suite` wiring._
144	- [x] ✅ Integration tests across all three datasource types — `backend/tests/integration/test_datasource_runs.py`: end-to-end suite runs through the **real** `run_service.execute_run` + **real Postgres** persistence, with **real GX execution** for the flat-file (`FlatFileCheckRunner`) and Unity Catalog (`UnityCatalogCheckRunner`) paths on an in-memory DataFrame (only the file download / table read is stubbed) — exercising the whole chain: `check.kind` dispatch → runner → GX validation → severity derivation → `Result` rows. Snowflake runs through a canned outcome (live warehouse is the deferred-smoke seam; its GX mapping is covered in `datasources/test_snowflake.py`). Asserts run `succeeded`, both Results persisted, severity derived (null-check → `fail`, row-count → `pass` with observed value). Plus a parametrized **adversarial-frame** run (numpy + pyarrow hostile columns via the shared battery) proving a column-agnostic monitor still runs + persists over a file whose data column is hostile. 7 tests.
```
